::::

:::: {#S6.SS9.SSS0.Px2 .section .ltx_paragraph}
##### Precision. {#precision. .ltx_title .ltx_title_paragraph}

::: {#S6.SS9.SSS0.Px2.p1 .ltx_para}
FP16 and BF16 achieve nearly identical throughput
(${\sim}$`<!-- -->`{=html}60M docs/s at $B{=}10$K), while FP32 is
1.6$\times$ slower due to doubled IO per element. FP16/BF16 also halve
memory, enabling 2$\times$ more documents per GPU.
:::
::::
:::::::

::::::::::: {#S6.SS10 .section .ltx_subsection}
### [6.10. ]{.ltx_tag .ltx_tag_subsection}End-to-End Retrieval Quality {#end-to-end-retrieval-quality .ltx_title .ltx_title_subsection}

::: {#S6.SS10.p1 .ltx_para}
A critical requirement for any scoring kernel is *exact* preservation of
retrieval quality: we are not proposing a new retrieval model, but an
optimized implementation of the existing MaxSim operator.
[TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS10.p1.1.2 .ltx_text .ltx_font_smallcaps} computes the
same mathematical operation as reference MaxSim (it only changes the
order of memory accesses, not the arithmetic), so rankings must be
identical up to floating-point rounding.
:::

:::::: {#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1 .section .ltx_paragraph}
##### MS MARCO Verification. {#ms-marco-verification. .ltx_title .ltx_title_paragraph}

::: {#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p1 .ltx_para}
We adopt a *re-ranking* setup following standard ColBERT evaluation
protocol ([khattab2020colbert](#bib.bib1){.ltx_ref}): we load ColBERTv2
(128-dim) and encode 200 MS MARCO dev queries with their top-1000 BM25
candidates (187,565 query-passage pairs total). We score all pairs with
both reference PyTorch MaxSim and [TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.2
.ltx_text .ltx_font_smallcaps} V2-MQ, then compare rankings. The maximum
absolute score difference across all pairs is $9\times 10^{-6}$, well
within floating-point rounding. The resulting retrieval metrics are
[identical]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.3 .ltx_text .ltx_font_bold} to six
decimal places:
:::

::: {#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2 .ltx_para}
  [Metric]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.1.1.1 .ltx_text .ltx_font_bold style="font-size:90%;"}   [Reference]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.1.2.1 .ltx_text .ltx_font_bold style="font-size:90%;"}   [TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.1.3.1 .ltx_text .ltx_font_bold .ltx_font_smallcaps style="font-size:90%;"}   [Match]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.1.4.1 .ltx_text .ltx_font_bold style="font-size:90%;"}
  ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  [MRR@10]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.2.1.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}                  [0.3399]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.2.2.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}                     [0.3399]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.2.3.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}                                          [Exact]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.2.4.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}
  [nDCG@10]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.3.1.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}                 [0.3866]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.3.2.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}                     [0.3866]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.3.3.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}                                          [Exact]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.3.4.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}
  [Recall@10]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.4.1.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}               [0.5617]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.4.2.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}                     [0.5617]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.4.3.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}                                          [Exact]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.4.4.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}
  [Recall@100]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.5.1.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}              [0.7214]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.5.2.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}                     [0.7214]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.5.3.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}                                          [Exact]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.5.4.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}
  [Recall@1000]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.6.1.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}             [0.7833]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.6.2.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}                     [0.7833]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.6.3.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}                                          [Exact]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.6.4.1 .ltx_text style="font-size:90%;"}
:::

::: {#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px1.p3 .ltx_para .ltx_noindent}
Note: these scores are computed on re-encoded embeddings over BM25
candidates (200 queries); the absolute values differ from ColBERTv2's
published numbers (MRR@10=0.397 on full dev set with pre-built index)
due to the re-encoding and candidate set differences. The key result is
that both scoring methods produce *identical* rankings.
:::
::::::

::::: {#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px2 .section .ltx_paragraph}
##### BEIR Cross-Domain Evaluation. {#beir-cross-domain-evaluation. .ltx_title .ltx_title_paragraph}

::: {#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px2.p1 .ltx_para}
To confirm that speedups are consistent across domains, we evaluate on
three BEIR benchmarks. [[Table]{.ltx_text .ltx_ref_tag} [13]{.ltx_text
.ltx_ref_tag}](#S6.T13 "In BEIR Cross-Domain Evaluation. ‣ 6.10. End-to-End Retrieval Quality ‣ 6. Evaluation ‣ TileMaxSim: IO-Aware GPU MaxSim Scoring with Dimension Tiling and Fused Product Quantization"){.ltx_ref}
shows that [TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1 .ltx_text
.ltx_font_smallcaps} produces identical nDCG@10 to the reference on all
datasets.
:::

id="S6.T13.6" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">BEIR cross-domain
evaluation. <span id="S6.T13.6.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">TileMaxSim</span> produces
<em>identical</em> retrieval quality to reference PyTorch MaxSim across
all domains. ColBERTv2 embeddings, 128-dim. All metrics match to full
floating-point precision.</span></figcaption>
</figure>

::: {#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px2.p2 .ltx_para}
We select these three BEIR datasets to span diverse corpus sizes (3.6K
to 50K documents), domains (biomedical claims, medical abstracts,
scientific literature), and query characteristics. The identical quality
across in-domain (MS MARCO) and out-of-domain (BEIR) benchmarks confirms
that [TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS10.SSS0.Px2.p2.1.1 .ltx_text
.ltx_font_smallcaps} is a *drop-in replacement* for any MaxSim scoring
implementation, preserving all quality characteristics of the underlying
retrieval model.
:::
:::::
:::::::::::

:::::::::::::::: {#S6.SS11 .section .ltx_subsection}
### [6.11. ]{.ltx_tag .ltx_tag_subsection}Comparison with CPU Engines {#comparison-with-cpu-engines .ltx_title .ltx_title_subsection}

::: {#S6.SS11.p1 .ltx_para}
We compare [TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS11.p1.1.1 .ltx_text .ltx_font_smallcaps}'s
scoring throughput against state-of-the-art CPU-based multi-vector
engines.
:::

::::::: {#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1 .section .ltx_paragraph}
##### WARP ([scheerer2025warp](#bib.bib4){.ltx_ref}). {#warp-scheerer2025warp. .ltx_title .ltx_title_paragraph}

::: {#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1.p1 .ltx_para}
WARP is the current fastest CPU engine for ColBERT scoring, with a
3$\times$ speedup over PLAID via residual compression and
SIMD-vectorized decompression+scoring.
:::

::: {#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1.p2 .ltx_para}
We directly benchmark WARP on the same machine used for
[TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.1 .ltx_text .ltx_font_smallcaps}
evaluation (Intel Xeon Gold 6448Y, single-threaded, DDR5). Using WARP's
official codebase with their recommended configuration
([nbits=2]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.2 .ltx_text .ltx_font_typewriter},
[nprobe=32]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.3 .ltx_text .ltx_font_typewriter},
[bound=196]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.4 .ltx_text .ltx_font_typewriter}),
we measure per-query latency on BEIR-NFCorpus (3,633 documents, 324
queries):
:::

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="S6.T14.5"
class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 14</span>. </span><span
id="S6.T14.6" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Directly measured
comparison of <span id="S6.T14.6.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">TileMaxSim</span> (GPU) vs. WARP
(CPU) scoring. WARP measured on the same node (Xeon Gold 6448Y,
single-threaded, DDR5-4800). Both systems use 2-bit quantized
representations. <span id="S6.T14.6.2"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">TileMaxSim</span> runs on H100
(HBM3, 3.35 TB/s peak).</span></figcaption>
</figure>

::: {#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1.p3 .ltx_para}
WARP's per-query latency of 88.7 ms is dominated by query encoding (96%,
T5-base inference). The actual scoring phase (decompression and matrix
construction) takes only 1.12 ms but operates on at most 196 candidates
per query (controlled by the [bound]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1.p3.1.1 .ltx_text
.ltx_font_typewriter} parameter). In contrast,
[TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1.p3.1.2 .ltx_text .ltx_font_smallcaps}
scores 500K candidates in 1.23 ms, yielding
[469$\times$]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1.p3.1.3 .ltx_text .ltx_font_bold} higher
scoring throughput (82M docs/s vs. 175K docs/s).
:::

::: {#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1.p4 .ltx_para}
This throughput gap stems from the 43$\times$ memory bandwidth advantage
of H100 HBM3 over DDR5, compounded by our kernel's 80% bandwidth
utilization through IO-aware tiling. We note that this comparison
isolates the scoring kernel: WARP is a complete retrieval pipeline while
[TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px1.p4.1.1 .ltx_text .ltx_font_smallcaps} is
a scoring primitive. The architectural takeaway is that GPU memory
bandwidth enables scoring entire corpora in milliseconds, making
brute-force multi-vector scoring practical as an alternative to
approximate search.
:::
:::::::

::::::: {#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px2 .section .ltx_paragraph}
##### Drop-in Integration with PLAID/ColBERTv2. {#drop-in-integration-with-plaidcolbertv2. .ltx_title .ltx_title_paragraph}

::: {#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px2.p1 .ltx_para}
To validate that [TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1 .ltx_text
.ltx_font_smallcaps} improves end-to-end retrieval latency (not just
kernel throughput in isolation), we integrate it as a drop-in
replacement for ColBERTv2/PLAID's scoring function
([colbert_score_packed]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.2 .ltx_text
.ltx_font_typewriter}). The integration requires no changes to candidate
generation, decompression, or any other pipeline stage; only the final
MaxSim scoring kernel is swapped. On the BEIR-NFCorpus PLAID index
(3,633 documents, 324 queries), the drop-in produces identical retrieval
quality (nDCG@10 matches exactly), confirming correctness.
:::

::: {#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px2.p2 .ltx_para}
At small candidate sets ($\sim$`<!-- -->`{=html}64 documents per query),
scoring is negligible and both kernels yield identical end-to-end
latency ($\sim$`<!-- -->`{=html}3.7 ms). The impact becomes dramatic at
realistic candidate set sizes:
:::

<td id="S6.T15.7.5.3" class="ltx_td ltx_align_right ltx_border_bb"><span
id="S6.T15.7.5.3.1" class="ltx_text"
style="font-size:90%;">1.21</span></td>
<td id="S6.T15.7.5.4" class="ltx_td ltx_align_right ltx_border_bb"><span
id="S6.T15.7.5.4.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">220<span
class="math inline">×</span></span></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="S6.T15.5"
class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 15</span>. </span><span
id="S6.T15.6" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">End-to-end impact
of replacing PLAID’s scoring kernel with <span id="S6.T15.6.1"
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">TileMaxSim</span>. Candidate
generation and decompression are identical; only the MaxSim scoring
changes. Quality is identical (exact MaxSim in both cases). <span
class="math inline"><em>N</em><sub><em>d</em></sub> = 128</span>, <span
class="math inline"><em>d</em> = 128</span>, FP16, H100.
<sup>†</sup>Verified identical nDCG@10 on BEIR-NFCorpus PLAID
pipeline.</span></figcaption>
</figure>

::: {#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px2.p3 .ltx_para}
At 100K candidates ($N_{d}{=}128$, $d{=}128$), PLAID's standard scoring
([D_packed @ Q.T]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px2.p3.1.1 .ltx_text
.ltx_font_typewriter} followed by
[StridedTensor]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px2.p3.1.2 .ltx_text .ltx_font_typewriter}
padding and reduction) takes 268 ms, dominating total pipeline latency.
Replacing this with [TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px2.p3.1.3 .ltx_text
.ltx_font_smallcaps} reduces scoring to 1.2 ms, cutting end-to-end
latency by 98%. A PLAID pipeline that previously required
$\sim$`<!-- -->`{=html}272 ms (4 ms candidate generation + 268 ms
scoring) now completes in $\sim$`<!-- -->`{=html}5 ms.
:::

::: {#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px2.p4 .ltx_para}
This has a direct implication for retrieval quality: PLAID and WARP
aggressively prune candidates (WARP scores $\leq$`<!-- -->`{=html}196
per query via its [bound]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px2.p4.1.1 .ltx_text
.ltx_font_typewriter} parameter) because CPU/naive-GPU scoring is
expensive. With [TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px2.p4.1.2 .ltx_text
.ltx_font_smallcaps}, scoring 100K candidates costs only 1.2 ms,
removing the need for aggressive pruning and enabling higher recall.
:::
:::::::

:::: {#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px3 .section .ltx_paragraph}
##### Why Not Just Use FAISS GPU? {#why-not-just-use-faiss-gpu .ltx_title .ltx_title_paragraph}

::: {#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px3.p1 .ltx_para}
FAISS ([faiss](#bib.bib21){.ltx_ref}) provides GPU-accelerated
single-vector similarity search via IVF-PQ and flat indices. However,
FAISS fundamentally operates on single vectors: it computes $q^{\top}d$
for single embeddings, not $\sum_{i}\max_{j}q_{i}^{\top}d_{j}$ for sets
of embeddings. Applying FAISS to MaxSim would require either (a)
flattening all token embeddings into a single index and performing
$N_{q}$ separate searches per query (losing the max-over-documents
structure), or (b) computing the full $N_{q}\times N_{d}$ similarity
matrix via batched GEMM and then reducing (which is exactly the naive
baseline we outperform by 6.5$\times$).
[TileMaxSim]{#S6.SS11.SSS0.Px3.p1.1.1 .ltx_text .ltx_font_smallcaps} is
purpose-built for the MaxSim operator's unique matmul-max-sum reduction
pattern.
:::
::::
::::::::::::::::
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

::::::: {#S7 .section .ltx_section}
## [7. ]{.ltx_tag .ltx_tag_section}Discussion {#discussion .ltx_title .ltx_title_section}

::: {#S7.p1 .ltx_para}
[TileMaxSim]{#S7.p1.1.1 .ltx_text .ltx_font_smallcaps} is a drop-in
scoring kernel ([[Table]{.ltx_text .ltx_ref_tag} [15]{.ltx_text
.ltx_ref_tag}](#S6.T15 "In Drop-in Integration with PLAID/ColBERTv2. ‣ 6.11. Comparison with CPU Engines ‣ 6. Evaluation ‣ TileMaxSim: IO-Aware GPU MaxSim Scoring with Dimension Tiling and Fused Product Quantization"){.ltx_ref}),
most beneficial when the corpus is GPU-resident and $B>1$K.
:::

::::: {#S7.SS1 .section .ltx_subsection}
### [7.1. ]{.ltx_tag .ltx_tag_subsection}Large-Scale Evaluation {#large-scale-evaluation .ltx_title .ltx_title_subsection}

::: {#S7.SS1.p1 .ltx_para}
On 100K real MS MARCO passages encoded with
ColBERTv2 ([santhanam2022colbertv2](#bib.bib2){.ltx_ref}), V2-MQ
achieves [71.6M docs/s]{#S7.SS1.p1.1.1 .ltx_text .ltx_font_bold} (70%
peak BW), with max score difference $2\times 10^{-6}$ vs. reference
PyTorch. [[Table]{.ltx_text .ltx_ref_tag} [16]{.ltx_text
.ltx_ref_tag}](#S7.T16 "In 7.1. Large-Scale Evaluation ‣ 7. Discussion ‣ TileMaxSim: IO-Aware GPU MaxSim Scoring with Dimension Tiling and Fused Product Quantization"){.ltx_ref}
extends to 500K documents.
:::

random).</span></figcaption>

::: {#S7.SS1.p2 .ltx_para}
Throughput saturates at 83M docs/s from 100K onward (81.4% peak BW). At
this rate, brute-force FP16 scoring of the full MS MARCO corpus (8.8M
passages) takes ${\sim}$`<!-- -->`{=html}107 ms on a single H100, or
${\sim}$`<!-- -->`{=html}27 ms sharded data-parallel across
4$\times$H100. PQ compression ([TileMaxSim-PQ]{#S7.SS1.p2.1.1 .ltx_text
.ltx_font_smallcaps}) instead reduces the per-GPU memory footprint so
the full corpus fits on one H100, at lower scoring throughput
(Table [[5]{.ltx_text
.ltx_ref_tag}](#S6.T5 "Table 5 ‣ 6.5. PQ Scoring Performance ‣ 6. Evaluation ‣ TileMaxSim: IO-Aware GPU MaxSim Scoring with Dimension Tiling and Fused Product Quantization"){.ltx_ref}).
:::
:::::
:::::::

:::: {#S8 .section .ltx_section}
## [8. ]{.ltx_tag .ltx_tag_section}Conclusion {#conclusion .ltx_title .ltx_title_section}

::: {#S8.p1 .ltx_para}
[TileMaxSim]{#S8.p1.1.1 .ltx_text .ltx_font_smallcaps} closes the
roofline gap for multi-vector retrieval from 5--18% to [80% of peak HBM
bandwidth]{#S8.p1.1.2 .ltx_text .ltx_font_bold} via IO-aware tiling,
achieving 220$\times$ over loop-based scoring, 6.6--8.5$\times$ over
[torch.compile]{#S8.p1.1.3 .ltx_text .ltx_font_typewriter}, and
469$\times$ over WARP (directly measured), with zero quality
degradation. At 83M docs/s on a single H100, it scores 100K candidates
in 1.2 ms, reducing ColBERTv2/PLAID pipeline latency by 98%.
[TileMaxSim-PQ]{#S8.p1.1.4 .ltx_text .ltx_font_smallcaps} extends
IO-aware scoring to PQ-compressed indices, reducing HBM traffic by
$\sim$`<!-- -->`{=html}31$\times$. Current limitations include
fixed-$N_{d}$ padding (38% token waste for variable-length corpora;
length-sorted batching recovers to 70M/s) and GPU-resident data
assumption.
:::

## Instructions for reporting errors

We are continuing to improve HTML versions of papers, and your feedback
helps enhance accessibility and mobile support. To report errors in the
HTML that will help us improve conversion and rendering, choose any of
the methods listed below:

- Click the \"Report Issue\"
  [(![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyByb2xlPSJwcmVzZW50YXRpb24iIHN0eWxlPSJkaXNwbGF5OiBpbmxpbmUtYmxvY2s7IHZlcnRpY2FsLWFsaWduOiBtaWRkbGU7IGZpbGw6IHZhcigtLXRleHQtY29sb3IpOyIgYXJpYS1oaWRkZW49InRydWUiIGhlaWdodD0iMWVtIiB2aWV3Ym94PSIwIDAgNjQwIDY0MCI+CiAgICAgICAgICAgIDxwYXRoIGQ9Ik0yMjQgMTYwQzIyNCAxMDcgMjY3IDY0IDMyMCA2NEMzNzMgNjQgNDE2IDEwNyA0MTYgMTYwTDQxNiAxNjMuNkM0MTYgMTc5LjMgNDAzLjMgMTkyIDM4Ny42IDE5MkwyNTIuNSAxOTJDMjM2LjggMTkyIDIyNC4xIDE3OS4zIDIyNC4xIDE2My42TDIyNC4xIDE2MHpNNTY5LjYgMTcyLjhDNTgwLjIgMTg2LjkgNTc3LjMgMjA3IDU2My4yIDIxNy42TDQ2NS40IDI5MC45QzQ3MC43IDI5OS44IDQ3NC43IDMwOS42IDQ3Ny4yIDMyMEw1NzYgMzIwQzU5My43IDMyMCA2MDggMzM0LjMgNjA4IDM1MkM2MDggMzY5LjcgNTkzLjcgMzg0IDU3NiAzODRMNDgwIDM4NEw0ODAgNDE2QzQ4MCA0MTguNiA0NzkuOSA0MjEuMyA0NzkuOCA0MjMuOUw1NjMuMiA0ODYuNEM1NzcuMyA0OTcgNTgwLjIgNTE3LjEgNTY5LjYgNTMxLjJDNTU5IDU0NS4zIDUzOC45IDU0OC4yIDUyNC44IDUzNy42TDQ2MS43IDQ5MC4zQzQzOC41IDUzNC41IDM5NS4yIDU2Ni41IDM0NCA1NzQuMkwzNDQgMzQ0QzM0NCAzMzAuNyAzMzMuMyAzMjAgMzIwIDMyMEMzMDYuNyAzMjAgMjk2IDMzMC43IDI5NiAzNDRMMjk2IDU3NC4yQzI0NC44IDU2Ni41IDIwMS41IDUzNC41IDE3OC4zIDQ5MC4zTDExNS4yIDUzNy42QzEwMS4xIDU0OC4yIDgxIDU0NS4zIDcwLjQgNTMxLjJDNTkuOCA1MTcuMSA2Mi43IDQ5NyA3Ni44IDQ4Ni40TDE2MC4yIDQyMy45QzE2MC4xIDQyMS4zIDE2MCA0MTguNyAxNjAgNDE2TDE2MCAzODRMNjQgMzg0QzQ2LjMgMzg0IDMyIDM2OS43IDMyIDM1MkMzMiAzMzQuMyA0Ni4zIDMyMCA2NCAzMjBMMTYyLjggMzIwQzE2NS4zIDMwOS42IDE2OS4zIDI5OS44IDE3NC42IDI5MC45TDc2LjggMjE3LjZDNjIuNyAyMDcgNTkuOCAxODYuOSA3MC40IDE3Mi44QzgxIDE1OC43IDEwMS4xIDE1NS44IDExNS4yIDE2Ni40TDIyNCAyNDhDMjM2LjMgMjQyLjkgMjQ5LjggMjQwIDI2NCAyNDBMMzc2IDI0MEMzOTAuMiAyNDAgNDAzLjcgMjQyLjggNDE2IDI0OEw1MjQuOCAxNjYuNEM1MzguOSAxNTUuOCA1NTkgMTU4LjcgNTY5LjYgMTcyLjh6IiAvPgogICAgICAgICAgPC9zdmc+))]{.mobile-only}

**Tip:** You can select the relevant text first, to include it in your
report.

Our team has already identified [the following
issues](https://github.com/arXiv/html_feedback/issues){.ltx_ref
target="_blank"}. We appreciate your time reviewing and reporting
rendering errors we may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us
improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability should not
be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support
in championing open access for all.

Have a free development cycle? Help support accessibility at arXiv! Our
collaborators at LaTeXML maintain a [list of packages that need
conversion](https://github.com/brucemiller/LaTeXML/wiki/Porting-LaTeX-packages-for-LaTeXML){.ltx_ref
target="_blank"}, and welcome [developer
contributions](https://github.com/brucemiller/LaTeXML/issues){.ltx_ref
target="_blank"}.
